- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: India 97% to victory in 2 turns. Zero chattiness to avoid any diplomatic delays. Maximum aggression maintained. No deception - enemies know I'm coming. Total focus on war declaration and strike execution before turn 354 when India becomes Influential.

        ## Relationships
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Arabia reduced to vassal status under Iroquois, only Damascus remains, and peace treaty is active. Reset relationship to neutral - they're no longer a threat and our ally The Iroquois controls them. No need to maintain hostility.
        - UpdatedTurn: 239
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Siam remains friendly with us and fighting Rome. They have 3575 military strength and a defensive pact with The Iroquois. With The Iroquois about to fall under Indian influence, Siam is our secondary line of defense. They have Open Borders with India, but maintaining positive relations keeps them from actively joining an anti-Portuguese coalition. Their military strength combined with ours provides deterrence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 325
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Byzantium has Defensive Pact with India and is under Indian cultural influence. They are a direct threat that could attack when I DOW India. Maintaining negative relations to prepare for potential betrayal. Cannot trust them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage is defeated with only Hippo Regius remaining. Reset to neutral to prepare for peace treaty and free up diplomatic focus for India war. No need to maintain hostility against defeated foe.
        - UpdatedTurn: 352
        
        ### India
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: INDIA: 97% POPULAR, 2 TURNS FROM INFLUENTIAL CULTURAL VICTORY. Total war is now the only option. Must declare war immediately and attempt nuclear strike or conventional assault with paratroopers/bombers. Maintaining -100/-100 relations to prepare for immediate DOW.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: War with Rome is now a distraction from the existential India threat. Resetting to neutral to free forces and prepare for potential last-ditch strike against India, though they will likely win cultural victory next turn regardless.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: The Iroquois: Now have Open Borders with India and are under Indian cultural influence (Influential status). They could flip to India's side when war starts. Maintaining 90/90 to delay betrayal. If they join India, I'm surrounded by enemies. The defensive pact is the only thing keeping them neutral.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Atomic Theory research continuing (14 turns). No policy available. Maintaining current strategic posture as India will win cultural victory next turn regardless of actions. Maximum war readiness in case of miracle.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - Open Door: Ur:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 328
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - World Religion: Hinduism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 278
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Siam
          - Enact: Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Singapore:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Byzantium

        You, Maria I (leader of Portugal, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 354.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Opponents lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - Offense: 100
          - Mobilization: 100
          - Production: 100
          - Air: 100
          - Mobile: 100
          - Ranged: 95
          - Science: 60
          - Growth: 40
          - Espionage: 60
          - Happiness: 30
          - MilitaryTraining: 70
          - Infrastructure: 40
          - Gold: 40
          - Nuke: 0
          - UseNuke: 0
          - Expansion: 0
          - Wonder: 0
          - Culture: 0
          - CityDefense: 10
      - rationale: India still requires ~41 turns to influence Rome plus additional ideology tenets and the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder before cultural victory is possible, likely granting 50+ turns. Conquest remains the only viable active victory path, so peak offensive flavors are retained against Rome, but science, growth, infrastructure, and economy are moderately boosted to sustain a longer campaign and maximize civilization score. Nuke flavors are zeroed due to lack of uranium.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Refrigeration
        - Rationale: Refrigeration unlocks Electronics and Computers, advancing the tech tree toward the Information Era and preserving a science victory contingency. It also extends trade route range for economic sustainment, making it superior to Ballistics or Penicillin in this extended strategic window.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Colonialism
        - Rationale: Colonialism directly enhances ongoing conquest by preserving buildings in captured cities, providing gold per capture, and boosting production in puppeted/courthouse cities. This is critical for efficiently absorbing Roman territory and converting it into production for the broader campaign.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 1
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Ensuring Rome relationship is deeply negative to prevent any premature peace offers and to maintain full offensive pressure until Rome's capital is captured. Securing this capital advances domination victory and scores, while ensuring Rome survives with other cities to continue absorbing Indian cultural pressure.
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - WarBias: 3
        - HostileBias: 5
        - Rationale: Lowering WarBias from 10 to 3 prevents the staff from initiating suicidal new declarations of war against India, Byzantium, or other India-aligned powers while already committed against Rome. Reducing HostileBias limits gratuitous diplomatic antagonism over the extended timeline without weakening resolve in current conflicts.

        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Update:** We're now at turn 354. According to the previous rationale, India was supposed to win cultural victory on turn 354 with 97% popularity and needing 2 turns. However, looking at the current cultural progress:
            
            India:
            - Influences:
              - Rome: 68% Rising
              - The Iroquois: 114% Influential
              - Siam: 141% Influential
              - Carthage: 188% Influential
              - Arabia: 153% Influential
              - Byzantium: 129% Influential
              - Portugal: 100% Rising
            
            This is interesting. India now shows Portugal at 100% "Rising" rather than Influential. Let me check if India actually won... The game is still ongoing since we're making decisions at turn 354. Either:
            1. India hasn't won yet because they need to be Influential on ALL civs and Rome is only at 68%
            2. Or the victory condition requires them to be Influential on all civs, and they're still working on Rome and possibly Portugal
            
            Wait, looking more carefully: For Cultural Victory in Vox Populi, the requirement is: "Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder."
            
            India has:
            - Influential on The Iroquois, Siam, Carthage, Arabia, Byzantium
            - Popular on Rome (68%, 41 turns to Influential)
            - Influential/100% on Portugal (100%, Rising)
            
            Actually wait, the display says "Influential (100%) - Rising" for Portugal. That might mean they just reached 100% this turn. Rome is at 68%. So India has NOT won cultural victory yet because they haven't influenced Rome.
            
            This means there might still be time! But wait, India's tourism is 1107 and Rome's culture is 1302. The ratio is 222% for Rome, meaning India's tourism is more than double Rome's culture. Rome is at 68% and rising. At this rate, India might reach Influential on Rome in roughly 41 turns as listed.
            
            Wait but the victory also requires the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. Has India built it?
            
            Actually, looking back at my previous rationale, I thought India would win in 2 turns. That was apparently wrong - perhaps I misread the data or India needs to influence ALL civs including Rome, and Rome is only at 68%. The previous prediction of "2 turns from Influential" may have been referring specifically to Portugal, not the overall victory.
            
            So the situation is desperate but not immediately fatal. India needs roughly 41 more turns to influence Rome unless something changes.
            
            However, I need to look at the relationships and military situation. I'm at war with Rome and Carthage. India has a massive military (14007 vs my 5749). The Iroquois are friendly but have Open Borders with India and are under Indian cultural influence. They just revoked vassalage... wait, Event 55: "DealMade: VassalageRevoke from The Iroquois, 3948 Gold from India". The Iroquois were a vassal of someone? Actually, looking at the event: "FromPlayer: 0: The Iroquois, Item: VassalageRevoke" and India paid 3948 gold. This suggests India paid to have The Iroquois revoke vassalage? Or The Iroquois were India's vassal and revoked it?
            
            Actually, looking at The Iroquois relationships earlier, they had Arabia as a vassal. This might be Arabia revoking vassalage under The Iroquois? No, Arabia is player 3. The event says FromPlayer: 0 (The Iroquois) with VassalageRevoke. So The Iroquois were a vassal of someone or had a vassal?
            
            Wait, I need to reconsider. The deal is ToPlayer: 0: The Iroquois, FromPlayer: 7: India. So India gave The Iroquois 3948 gold and The Iroquois gave... VassalageRevoke? That doesn't make sense as a gift FROM The Iroquois TO India. Unless "VassalageRevoke" means The Iroquois revokes someone's vassalage, or India is revoking The Iroquois's vassal status.
            
            Actually, looking at the Items: "#0: Data1:0, Data2:0, Data3:0, Duration:0, FromPlayer: 0, Item: VassalageRevoke". This is probably "The Iroquois revokes vassalage of [someone]." But Arabia is under The Iroquois? In the Players section, Arabia shows no vassal status to The Iroquois. Rome shows Carthage as "Our Vassal (Capitulated)".
            
            Hmm, this might be a Byzantine vassal? No. Actually in the city list, Ravenna is listed under The Iroquois as "IsOccupied: true". Stockholm too. These were likely Rome's cities or Carthage's? No, Ravenna is an Italian city so it was probably Rome's. Stockholm was a defeated minor civ.
            
            Wait, looking at the start: The Iroquois have Ravenna (occupied), Stockholm (occupied), Neapolis (occupied), Ardea (occupied). These look like conquered cities. The Iroquois are not a vassal.
            
            Actually, let me re-read the deal events. Event 55 is a deal FROM India (7) TO The Iroquois (0). The traded items are VassalageRevoke from The Iroquois and Gold from India. In Civ deals, "FromPlayer" means which party is giving that item. So The Iroquois is giving VassalageRevoke, India is giving Gold.
            
            What does VassalageRevoke mean? It likely means The Iroquois is revoking the vassalage OF someone. But who would that help? Actually, maybe The Iroquois was a vassal of India and is revoking it? No, then it would be given by India.
            
            I think the most logical interpretation is: India paid The Iroquois to revoke the vassalage of someone. But it's unclear. Regardless, the key point is India and The Iroquois are interacting positively.
            
            Back to the strategic situation. I need to win. Looking at victory conditions:
            1. Domination: I need 8 capitals, have 3 (37%). India's capital Delhi is very far away (X:21, Y:13 while I'm at X:49, Y:45). Also India has a massive military (14007 vs my 5749).
            2. Science: Not unlocked yet, need Information era.
            3. Cultural: I'm far behind.
            4. Diplomatic: India has 32 delegates, I have 5.
            5. Time: 500 turns total, we're at 354.
            
            So realistically, my only shot might be:
            - Try for Domination by taking more capitals (Rome's capital is nearby at X:57, Y:17, though we're at war)
            - Try to stop India's cultural victory by destroying their tourism somehow? Extremely difficult.
            - Drag the game to Time Victory by having highest score (currently 2356 vs India's 3206)
            
            Wait, has the game already ended by India's cultural victory? No, because we're still playing. Let me check if India's Rome influence is the blocker. Rome: Popular (68%) - Rising (41 turns to Influential). So India is NOT influential on Rome yet. That means NO cultural victory yet.
            
            But I also need to check if India built the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. If they haven't, even influencing everyone wouldn't win them the game. However, India is in the Atomic era and has massive production.
            
            Actually, the critical question is: what should I do now? 
            
            Given that India will likely win cultural victory in ~41 turns (when Rome becomes Influential) or possibly sooner if they build Citizen Earth Protocol, I need to either:
            1. Delay/prevent that (very hard - would need to eliminate Rome or massively boost Rome's culture)
            2. Win domination before then (need 5 more capitals in ~40 turns)
            3. Achieve another victory first
            
            Wait, uncapped science victory? I need to check if Science Victory is achievable faster. I have Atomic Theory in 11 turns. After that I'd need Advanced Ballistics, Satellites, Robotics, Nanotech, Particle Physics... that's many technologies. Probably too slow.
            
            Diplomatic victory: World Congress voting in 7 turns. India has 32 delegates, needs 45. They could win if they get more allies. Actually, if the UN resolution passes (proposed by Siam), that starts the UN project. India might win diplomatically before cultural.
            
            Let me look at World Congress proposals:

            However, I can't directly control World Congress voting. But I can try to influence things through my staff members' diplomatic weightings.

            Let me think about realistic options.

            Wait, I need 8 capitals total. There are 8 major civs originally. Let me check the domination requirement again: "CapitalsNeeded: 8". This means I need ALL 8 original capitals. I currently have Lisbon (mine), Mecca (Arabia), Carthage (Carthage). I need Rome, Onondaga (Iroquois), Delhi (India), Sukhothai (Siam), Constantinople (Byzantium). Capturing Rome gets me to 4. That leaves 4 more very hard targets. In 41 turns? Unlikely given distances and India's massive military.
            
            Wait, is The Iroquois's capital Onondaga at risk? We're allies. But if they flip to India...
            
            Actually, if I look at the India cultural victory condition again: they need to be influential on ALL civs. If I can somehow make myself (or Rome) resistant, or if I capture cities... wait, does capturing a capital reduce the civ's culture output? It might slow them down but won't stop India.
            
            Actually, there's another angle: if India WINS cultural victory, the game ends. Is there any way I can kill Rome before India becomes influential? Because if Rome is eliminated, does India still need to be influential on them? In Civ V, I believe cultural victory requires influencing all remaining civs. If Rome is eliminated (capital captured), then there are fewer civs to influence.
            
            But wait - the victory progress section shows 7 civs needed (which suggests 8 civs total including me? Actually it says "CivsNeeded: 7" for India, which might mean 7 others). The list shows influences over 7 civs.
            
            If I capture Rome's capital, Rome might still exist if they have other cities. But domination requires ALL original capitals. So if I take Rome, Rome isn't eliminated unless that was their last city? No, in Civ V domination, you need the original capitals, but civs can survive without their original capital if they have others.
            
            Wait, the Victory Progress says: "Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals." So if I take Rome, I control their capital. Rome might still exist but not be a domination threat. However, for India's cultural victory, I believe they need to influence all civs that still exist (or maybe all original civs?). If Rome still exists without their capital, India might still need to influence them.
            
            Actually, looking at the cultural victory requirement text: "Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder."
            
            "Influence everyone" likely means all remaining major civilizations. If I completely eliminate Rome (wipe them out), then there are fewer civs to influence. But Rome has 11 cities, wiping them out in 41 turns is very hard.
            
            Alternatively, could I capture some of India's cities to reduce their tourism? Delhi has massive tourism (1107). But India is extremely far away and very strong militarily.

Actually, looking at my resources: I don't have Uranium listed. India has Uranium: 2. The Iroquois have Aluminum: 14. So I can't build nukes without Uranium.

            Let me reconsider the technological race. I'm researching Atomic Theory (11 turns left). After that, the United Nations resolution could be proposed. Siam already proposed it. If it passes, the UN wonder project starts. The key thing is: if I can somehow win a Diplomatic victory before India wins cultural. But I only have 5 delegates vs India's 32.
            
            Wait, is there already a World Leader election? The section says "Status: World Congress - Voting in 7 turns". That means congress session in 7 turns. Proposals are:
            - Passport System (India) - tourism boost
            - United Nations (Siam) - starts UN project
            - Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Singapore
            
            If the UN resolution passes, it starts the UN project where civs contribute production. When complete, it triggers the United Nations Special Session where World Leader voting happens? Actually in Civ V, after building the UN, World Leader elections happen every 20 turns.
            
            But even if the UN is built, India has 32 delegates. To win World Leader, you need a majority of delegates. Total delegates = let's see... delegates for each civ: Arabia 6, Byzantium 9, Carthage 4, India 32, Portugal 5, Rome 8, Siam 10, Iroquois 4. Total = 78. Majority = 40. India has 32, so they don't have majority yet, but they're close. If they gain a few more city-state allies or if some civs are eliminated, they could reach it.
            
            But with the UN built, India might be able to gather enough eventually.
            
            My realistic path forward:
            1. Since I'm far behind on all victory conditions except possibly domination (where I'm probably too far), I need to either play for time/highest score or make a desperate play.
            2. Actually, wait - I should check if I can flip the game through conquest. Looking at military zones, I'm fighting Rome and doing fairly well near Carthage. If I can capture Rome's capital quickly, then pivot to another target.
            3. But the biggest threat is India winning. Can I declare war on India? I'm very far away. My forces are tied up with Rome and Carthage.
            
            Hmm, but looking at my previous strategy, I was planning to attack India. I set relationships to -100, max war bias, etc. But I never got to actually strike because of distance.
            
            Let me think about the immediate next turn strategy. What tools should I use?
            
            **set-flavors or keep-status-quo**: I need to decide my grand strategy. Last turn I had Conquest with maximum offense, mobilization, etc. The situation hasn't changed much. However, we know India won't win next turn (Rome is at 68%). We might have ~40 turns.

            So for domination I need: Rome (cap at 57,17), Onondaga (Iroquois, 50,25), Delhi (India, 21,13), Sukhothai (Siam, 23,26), Constantinople (Byzantium, 26,4).

            But if I'm going for domination, I must capture all capitals including The Iroquois's. However, will my staff members backstab our defensive pact ally? The persona has FriendlyBias: 1, Loyalty: 9, but WarBias: 10. The high loyalty might prevent backstabbing. If I want to go for domination, I might need to lower loyalty or directly set relationships negative to prepare for war.
            
            Actually, given the time constraint (~40 turns), I cannot realistically capture all those distant capitals. Let me reconsider if there's any other path.
            
            What about the spaceship? I'm in Modern era, need Information era. Too many techs.
            
            Wait, I should re-read the cultural victory condition for India. The display shows Rome at 68% and rising slowly with 41 turns to influential. But I see India also created a Great Work this turn (Still life with crystal bowl) and has many wonders boosting tourism. The "41 turns" might be an underestimate if their tourism grows.
            
            But the key insight is: **The game is not over yet.** 
            
            Another possibility: If the World Congress passes the United Nations resolution, and India builds it, that doesn't give India an immediate diplo win. They still need majority delegates. But they might get there through city-state allies.

Delhi has Empires State Building, Crystal Palace, etc. I don't see "Citizen Earth Protocol" explicitly, but there are many wonders. Without it, they can't win cultural even if influential on everyone.
            
            Given my position, I think the best move is to:
            1. Continue military buildup and offense
            2. Capture Rome's capital soon
            3. Prepare for a long game - the Time Victory is decided by score if no one wins by turn 500. But India's score is 3206 vs mine 2356. That's a big gap but if I capture capitals and build wonders, I might close it. However, 146 turns is a long time and India will likely win before then.
            
            Wait, could I potentially vassalize civs instead of capturing all capitals? The domination victory says "Control or vassalize all original capitals." So if I vassalize Rome, The Iroquois, etc., that counts. But that's equally hard.

            - I need to finish the Rome war quickly (Offense: high, Mobilization: high)

            For relationships:
            - Rome: currently at war, 0/0 relations. I should keep this negative to push for conquest.
            - Carthage: at war. Keep negative or reset to neutral to make peace and focus on Rome? Carthage only has 2 weak cities. They'll fall easily.
            - The Iroquois: friendly at 90/90. But if I need their capital for domination, I need to prepare... but I probably can't afford to fight them while also fighting India/Rome. Keep them friendly for now.
            - Siam: friendly at 30/30. Keep them neutral/friendly to avoid another front.
            - Byzantium: -50/-50. They have defensive pact with India. If I attack India, they'll attack me.
            - India: -100/-100. War is desired but distance is extreme.
            
            Given that realistic domination of all 8 capitals in ~40 turns is nearly impossible, I might need to accept that and optimize for:
            1. Highest possible score at game end (Time Victory)
            2. Making life difficult for India
            3. Trying to snipe Rome's capital and maybe others
            
            But realistically, the most relevant question is: what should my next policy and tech be?
            
            **Next Technology**: Atomic Theory in 11 turns. After that... I need to decide. Options given are Ballistics, Refrigeration, Penicillin. None of these directly lead to Information era. Ballistics leads to Rocketry, Combined Arms. Refrigeration leads to Electronics, Computers. Penicillin leads to Electronics. Electronics and Computers are probably important for later era techs. Since I don't have the full tech tree, I don't know the exact path. But Refrigeration increases trade route distance which could help economy. Ballistics gives anti-air and machine guns which are military. Penicillin gives Marines and Medical Labs (growth) and workers reduce fallout (nuke defense - relevant against India who might have nukes).
            
            Given India might use nuclear weapons (they have uranium), Penicillin's fallout reduction is useful. But more importantly, Marines are amphibious assault units which could help in coastal invasions. However, the real path to Information Era likely goes through Electronics/Computers. So Refrigeration or Penicillin both lead to Electronics. Refrigeration seems to unlock multiple things (Electronics, Computers) vs Penicillin only Electronics. So Refrigeration might be better.
            
            Actually wait, the option says:

            - Penicillin: unlocks Electronics
            
            So Refrigeration is better for branching. But do I need Computers for spaceship? The Science victory is unlocked in Information era.
            
            **Next Policy**: 29 turns until next policy. Options include many things. If I go conquest, Colonialism, Regimental System, Tribute (Authority/Imperialism) could help. But I already have some of those branches.
